# Vietnamese syllable pairs.
# Format: <count><TAB><first> <second>[<TAB>seg]
#
# Counts are occurrences in a Vietnamese Wikipedia dump prefix,
# counted by tools/count_bigrams.py over 67.7 million adjacent
# pairs. Entries at the floor count were carried over from the
# hand-picked table that preceded this one: the corpus did not
# rank them, and it under-represents ordinary domestic
# vocabulary - banh cuon, ban ghe, bat dua - because an
# encyclopedia has little use for it.
#
# Two readers, two sizes, one table.
#
# Word segmentation INVENTS a cut with every pair it is allowed,
# so it reads only the pairs marked seg. Measured against the
# shipped English lexicon and the syllable dictionary:
#
#     pairs    English words cut   syllables cut
#     2,036            0                2     (hand-picked)
#     6,312            1                8
#    16,951            3               19
#    30,731           12               36
#
# Disambiguation is only asked once the corrector is already
# stuck between real words, so a rarer pair opens a case rather
# than inventing one, and it reads the whole table. Share of
# those resolved, against a sample covering 93.5% of running
# text, wrong answers in brackets:
#
#     pairs      Telex          VNI
#     6,312     49.0% [0.4]   47.4% [0.3]
#    11,106     57.6% [0.6]   56.6% [0.4]
#    20,880     65.7% [0.7]   65.5% [0.5]
#    30,742     70.2% [0.8]   70.5% [0.5]
#    60,532     76.3% [0.8]   78.0% [0.5]
#
# So: thirty thousand in the table, the commonest six thousand
# marked seg, and every hand-picked pair marked too - the corpus
# ranks those last, and they are the ones a person chose.
